‘Because he shows no weakness to anyone.’

Even when breath reaches his throat, when he’s exhausted to the point a twitch would topple him, even when his neck is at risk, Jang Ilso never stops playing the mighty self.

There were countless crises.

Even in utterly despairing moments, by refusing to lose the persona he wished to present, Jang Ilso came this far.

He has written legend beyond tale and now approaches the realm of faith.

Jang Ilso is such a person. So then...

Wouldn’t Chung Myung, who resembles Jang Ilso, be the same?

That unshakeable solidity that seems impossible to topple, that strength showing no falter even under impact, that grim reaper-like manner of slicing enemies aside...

Maybe it is an image forged through desperate artifice?

Is it not a visage he is determined to show, whether to allies or enemies?

Just as Jang Ilso endlessly shapes himself, might Chung Myung be doing the same?

‘He can’t not be tired.’

He fought the most people.

He expended the most internal qi.

He bled the most blood and bore the greatest burdens.

From that Southern Island to here, Chung Myung carried and endured all those things.

He cannot possibly not have broken down.

A human of blood and flesh cannot endure endlessly.

If he were to collapse, he should already have collapsed.

But at some point everyone began to believe otherwise.

They say Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, the man called Chung Myung, will never fall.

Both allies and even Gwi Yang who hunted him, and even Ho Ga-Myung who planned all this, half-doubted until the last moment.

It’s a phenomenon akin to faith.

An absoluteness that neither friend nor foe doubts.

This is a brilliant sculpture Chung Myung painstakingly etched into the martial world over years.

But now Ho Ga-Myung had clearly confirmed something.

That monster is, after all, human.

A fragile human of blood and flesh.

Ho Ga-Myung spoke as if driving a stake into his certainty.

“He’s already at his limit.”

Gwi Yang, with a baffled look, asked Ho Ga-Myung.

“What?”

“I confirmed his feet are dragging.”

“That’s all the reason you need?”

“It’s enough. More than enough.”

Ho Ga-Myung’s eyes gleamed coldly.

“Wild beasts do not show pain until right before death. They instinctively know the weak will die.”

“.......”

“He’s not a beast, but in the end he’s no different. He must be strong. He must not fall. He must always remain calm and steadfast. Yet this means he failed to do that.”

Ho Ga-Myung stated with certainty.

“That means he’s already collapsing.”

A hint of puzzlement appeared in Gwi Yang’s gaze.

He scrutinized Chung Myung again.

No matter how long he watched, it wasn’t easy to accept.

Chung Myung remained as ruthless and monstrous as when first seen.

Moreover, the spectacle he produced was no different from before.

Members of the Ten Thousand People Clan were being cut down as they charged.

But...

‘If you reason it out, it’s not entirely wrong.’

Was there any time to catch his breath?

He likely had no proper time to sleep, let alone to cultivate his qi.

And yet how many had already died by that sword in the meantime?

Even the easy task of reaping motionless rice with a well-sharpened sickle becomes backbreaking after a day of repetition.

But slicing people repeatedly for days on end without rest—how could one not grow tired?

The conclusion is clear: it’s absolutely impossible.

Think about it for a moment and it’s obvious.

The Ten Thousand People Clan traversed a distance that took days even without a single full battle, fighting along the way and even protecting those who followed them.

Yet before hearing Ho Ga-Myung, Gwi Yang had believed Chung Myung showed no fatigue at all.

“...It feels like being possessed by a ghost.”

“Don’t blame yourself. It’s not that you’re stupid—he’s extraordinary.”

Gwi Yang licked his dry lips from tension.

To be honest, he still wasn’t certain.

There was a large gap between what he understood intellectually and what he saw and felt.

‘It’s the word of a commander.’

But it was the word of the Mind-Probing Inquisitor, Ho Ga-Myung.

If he said so, wasn’t it worth testing?

“What are you hesitating about?”

“...Hmm?”

“Earn your keep.”

Gwi Yang couldn’t help but laugh at Ho Ga-Myung’s words.

Frankly, this wasn’t to his taste.

Gwi Yang normally preferred a more leisurely hunt.

He liked to savor the sight of wounded game when it could no longer resist and wheezed, drawing it out before ending it.

But if forced to choose, he didn’t particularly dislike the tremor when plunging a blade into the heart of still-strong prey.

“If it’s an order, gladly.”

And anyway, he had no intention of letting them leave this gorge peacefully.

It’s merely the difference between a fight to end a life and a fight to wound.

Ssshhh.

As Gwi Yang drew his sword, the Blood Sword Troops members waiting nearby closed in behind him as if eager.

“I’ll ask one thing first.”

Ho Ga-Myung shot a displeased glance at Gwi Yang’s remark.

It was clear he didn’t want to waste time on needless talk.

But Gwi Yang asked again despite knowing that.

“Target?”

“The obvious answer.”

Ho Ga-Myung’s gaze fixed on Chung Myung.

“Mount Hua’s Sword Saint. If we can take down that one bastard, the others don’t matter.”

“Clean one.”

Gwi Yang gave a thick smile and stepped toward the gorge.

“Let’s carry it out.”

He stamped his foot into the ground.

He stamped over the shoulders of those densely lined before him and shot forward like a meteor.

Honestly, it wasn’t his preferred terrain.

A single path is burdensome for those chasing from behind.

The hunted can clearly gauge the pursuer’s position, and in such places there are certainly more disadvantages than advantages.

Moreover, in a rocky gorge like this they couldn’t use the Blood Sword Squad’s specialty: attacks that defy common sense.

So all that remained was to collide with pure force.

But precisely because of that...

‘It will become clear.’

Gwi Yang’s body tore through the air as he advanced.

The narrow contours of the gorge on both sides warped at will.

In that twisted world, only the figure of Chung Myung straight ahead remained clear and distinct.

Shuaaaaaa!

Chung Myung’s cold eyes, which were cleaving through the bodies of the Ten Thousand People Clan, met Gwi Yang’s boiling gaze head-on in the air.

“Mount Hua’s Sword Saint!”

Fwoooosh!

Gwi Yang’s sword sliced the air as he surged toward Chung Myung.

A formidable speed, and a single strike carrying tremendous force to match.

Chung Myung contorted his eyes and stamped the ground.

He then powerfully countered Gwi Yang’s incoming blade.

Kraaaang!

The clash of metal burst like an explosion.

Red and blue sword qi flew from the two blades like fireworks scattering in all directions.

“Ha!”

An indescribable heat flared in Gwi Yang’s eyes.

An intense sensation in his wrist.

It must mean the prey he sought was still very much alive.

But Gwi Yang hesitated not at all and unleashed a series of strikes.

Parararak!

The sharp, thin swords flexed and lashed like dozens of venomous serpents at Chung Myung.

The strikes bit at him as if alive, tearing at his body.

In response, Chung Myung’s sword also released dozens of sword illusions.

The writhing tips of Gwi Yang’s blade and the swift, linear point of Chung Myung’s sword collided dozens of times in the space of a mere breath.

Kakagagagagang!

A clamor like hundreds of iron beads striking a steel plate rang out.

Each strike brimmed with a desperate will and killing intent to slay the opponent.

Kwagagagak!

The moment their sword visions entwined, Gwi Yang’s charging body was thrown back.

The thin swords could not withstand the power channeled in the Dark Fragrant Plum Blossom Sword.

Fwoooosh!

Chung Myung’s flying blade sliced long from beneath Gwi Yang’s eye down to his earlobe.

The area below the earlobe was cut away and blood spouted in droplets.

But at that moment, Gwi Yang’s sword style abruptly changed.

He instantaneously poured massive internal power into a downward strike.

Chung Myung met the descending blade without yielding.

Kwoaaaang!

Inner energy collided head-on, and shockwaves radiated in all directions.

Those swept by the sudden storm were flung back and tumbled.

But the two at the center of that fearsome impact did not retreat an inch; they kept pressing and testing each other’s blades.

Gak! Gagagagagak!

The sound of steel scraping echoed.

Bruddruk!

But more distinct to their ears was the sound of hands gripping the swords twisting under strain.

With neither yielding, their gazes clashed fiercely across the blades pushing at each other.

“Kugh kugh.”

Gwi Yang’s gaze, which had been pressing down on Chung Myung, suddenly dropped downward.

Precisely, toward Chung Myung’s leg.

He could see Chung Myung’s garments around his legs steadily darkening with wetness.

Blood was welling up from within.

“Kugh kugh kugh kugh. That leg...”

A gleam of triumph filled Gwi Yang’s eyes.

“Seems the wound hasn’t healed?”

“.......”

“Huh? Mount Hua’s Sword Saint!”

Kwoaaang! Gwi Yang forced his sword down toward Chung Myung’s injured leg with all his might.

Chung Myung’s body wavered and was thrown backward for a moment.

With half his face soaked in blood, Gwi Yang let out a wild grin and surged forward.

With the ferocity of a predator that has found wounded prey.

Yu Yiseol’s pupils trembled.

‘Blood?’

She belatedly noticed that Chung Myung’s leg, flung backward, was wet.

Of course, blood on him would be nothing unusual.

There is no way not to be splattered with blood on a battlefield.

But it was enough that she could see it continuing to soak through.

If it were enemy blood, it should have slowly begun to congeal.

That was clearly blood spurting from Chung Myung’s leg.

‘Wound!’

It must be the wound he took on the coast of the Southern Island.

The very wound they said would heal as long as he didn’t push himself too hard.

She had forgotten. No—she had been forced to forget.

Chung Myung had always fought despite such warnings, and there had never been any real problem.

So she assumed it would be the same this time.

Just as it had been up until now.

In an instant, Chung Myung flipped his body and thrust his foot toward the ground.

The moment the wounded leg bore his weight and stood taut, his pant leg suddenly puffed outward.

It was clear that blood spouting from the wound was striking the fabric from the inside.

Yu Yiseol froze for a moment.

Even so, Chung Myung charged straight at Gwi Yang without a moment’s hesitation.

“No...!”

Kaaaaaang!

Another violent collision erupted.

The aftershock swept over Yu Yiseol.

In the fierce wind that seemed like it could split her face, she saw Chung Myung’s back.

His back was trembling.

She realized anew.

Even after being told not to overexert himself, Chung Myung had never rested a moment and had kept fighting.

He had used that leg to scale the cliff and hold back the enemy.

There was no way it could be fine. It shouldn’t be fine.

So why had she believed he would be all right?

At that moment, Blood Sword Troops members, like red shadows, surged one after another toward Chung Myung’s head.

“Sajil!”

Yu Yiseol let out a scream that was almost a shriek.

Bang!

She stamped the ground desperately, but it was already too late.

Usually so rarely stirred emotionally, she couldn’t handle the torrent of feeling crashing over her now.

“Haap!”

Tang Pae’s reactions—unaware of Chung Myung’s condition—were inevitably quicker.

Swaaaak!

The dagger shot forward at light speed toward the Blood Sword Troops members charging Chung Myung.

Although the dagger moved incredibly fast, it was blocked by enemy blades before it could touch flesh and ricocheted away.

For a moment it only served to make the enemies hesitate.

Under normal circumstances, that would have been more than enough.

But not for the Chung Myung of now.

Paaaat!

The thin swords of the Blood Sword Troops members lunged at Chung Myung’s upper body like vipers.

Chung Myung snapped his torso back and avoided those blades.

But in that instant, Gwi Yang’s sword, curved like a long blade, wrapped around the Dark Fragrant Plum Blossom Sword and yanked it forward in one motion.

Kwoong!

To avoid being dragged away, Chung Myung stamped the ground forcefully without thinking.

But he couldn’t put enough force into it, and his body was pulled slightly forward.

Suk!

The countless thin swords that flew past slashed across Chung Myung’s body.

His garments were cut and red blood spurted up.

Paaaaat!

Yu Yiseol, her face twisted like a fiend’s, sprang up like a hawk.

A rapid rain of sword strikes poured onto the Blood Sword Troops members.

Kaang! Kaang!

But these opponents were not like the ones she had faced before.

If they had been ordinary members of the Ten Thousand People Clan, she could have cut them down, but the Blood Sword Troops members, though pushed back, managed to block all of Yu Yiseol’s strikes.

Swaaaak!

The pushed-back Blood Sword Troops members did not stop at defense; they thrust their swords toward her.

An azure sword qi at the tips was released in an instant, as if to pierce her whole body.

Thak!

Kicking at the air to flip her feet, Yu Yiseol narrowly evaded all of the incoming sword qi’s.

But even while dealing with those dangerous sword qi’s, her attention was fixed not on the Blood Sword Troops members but on Chung Myung below.

‘Sajil!’

Paaaaat!

While Yu Yiseol exchanged brief combos with the enemies, dozens of sword strikes continued back and forth below.

Blade met blade and fragments of qi burst forth one after another.

It was obvious how much burden the powerful clashes of qi must be placing on Chung Myung’s body.

“This...!”

Paat!

Pushing off the air and diving to the ground, Yu Yiseol launched herself forward the instant her feet touched the earth.

But before she could even reach Chung Myung, other Blood Sword Troops members popped up to Gwi Yang’s left and right and above, taking aim at Chung Myung.

Yu Yiseol thrust her sword with fierce determination.

The blade that flashed past Chung Myung’s face barely knocked aside one of the incoming thin swords.

But the remaining three blades were still Chung Myung’s to face; the Blood Sword Troops members’ swords streaked swiftly toward his neck.

Kwaaaa!

Gwi Yang, thinking he’d seized an opportunity, enveloped himself in tremendous sword qi and, in one breath, aimed at Chung Myung’s waist.

It was a moment of life or death.

A swift lightning-like thrust and a slash so brazen one could hardly imagine blocking it.

This clean, perfect pincer attack seemed ready to carve Chung Myung’s body to pieces.

If he didn’t step back, his waist would be sliced; if he retreated, he’d risk being impaled.

Chung Myung’s choice was simple.

The place most familiar to him.

Kwaang!

He drove off the ground and a thunderous blast erupted.

Before the blast could reach anyone’s ears, Chung Myung shot forward like a shell, plunging against Gwi Yang’s chest as if embracing it.

Gwi Yang’s all-out swing cut emptily through Chung Myung’s back, and the desperate thrust pierced only air.

Kuuuuung!

Driving into and pushing off Gwi Yang’s chest, Chung Myung explosively unleashed dozens of streams of sword qi.

Seeing the countless sword qi’s rise like the quills of an angry porcupine, the Blood Sword Troops members widened their eyes.

Kwad-deudeuk! Kwaduk!

Numerous wind-holes were pierced in the bodies of the Blood Sword Troops members.

Those whose throats were cut open to the point they could have screamed died without even a groan.

Yet even in that, Gwi Yang proved he was on another level from the other Blood Sword Squad members.

Kagagagagang!

Having deflected dozens of sword qi’s, Gwi Yang gave no pause and surged at Chung Myung again.

A blue murderous aura flowed from his eyes exposed above the mask.

Kwaaang!

Chung Myung’s blade and Gwe-ryang’s blade clashed again.

At that moment, Gwe-ryang’s left hand moved.

Like lightning, he drew a leather scabbard from his thigh and fired the willow-leaf blade toward Chung Myung’s face.

Suk!

Chung Myung jerked his head back quickly, but the sharp willow-leaf blade sliced long across his lower jaw.

Skin split and droplets of blood spattered.

“This...!”

Chung Myung snapped his head forward like a battering ram and used the recoil to violently shove the clashing blades away.

Kwoong!

The moment Gwi Yang’s body was jolted back, Chung Myung thrust his sword fully forward.

Pararararack!

With a sound like silk fluttering in a gale, the tip of the Dark Fragrant Plum Sword began to tremble.

From that quivering blade tip, dozens of vivid Plum Blossoms bloomed like an apparition.

At the sight of that blossom, Gwi Yang immediately swung his sword down into the air.

Three claw-like sword qi’s released from the thin swords collided with the Plum Blossoms that had barely begun to bloom.

Kagagang!

The unbloomed blossoms collided with the fierce sword qi’s and lost their forms.

But one of the three sword qi’s Gwi Yang released pierced through the forest of blossoms and still had enough force to lodge itself in Chung Myung’s flank.

Suk!

Chung Myung’s side split longwise and a gush of blood welled up.

But even then, Chung Myung’s blade did not stop.

Whaaaaaak!

Blooming and blooming again, the Plum Blossoms filled the gorge in an instant and billowed with the coming wind.

The scattering petals flew at Gwi Yang, who was hastily retreating, as if in fury.

This gorge was too narrow to evade the Plum Blossom Shower that Chung Myung had truly unleashed.

In terrain where he couldn’t pull back or roll aside, the blossoms filling every view were pure despair.

It was the same for Gwi Yang; no matter that he was the Blood Sword Troop’s Captain, avoiding all of this sword energy would be difficult.

But Gwi Yang did not choose that difficult path.

Grab!

Bending his waist and reaching behind him, Gwi Yang grabbed the shoulder of a Blood Sword Troops member who had run to support him as he was being pushed back.

And without a moment’s hesitation, he snatched him up and threw him toward the onrushing Plum Blossom sword qi.

“Ca-Captain!”

Gwi Yang did not stop there; with an almost demonic grip he threw anyone he could seize.

Three in all.

The Blood Sword Squad members, turned into meat shields to block the incoming Plum Blossom sword qi’s, widened their eyes in bewilderment.

At that moment, no one could tell whether the despair blooming in their eyes was fear or a sense of betrayal.

“Kuaaaargh!”

The Plum Blossom sword qi’s tore through the three Blood Sword Squad members’ bodies.

Before the agony of being sliced by hundreds of blades, even the most diligently amassed cultivation was useless.

Whiiiiing!

As the wind blew once more, the blossoms that had filled the world vanished cleanly like an illusion.

When the blossoms disappeared, all that remained were three corpses of Blood Sword Squad members, their bodies riddled with wounds and utterly empty.

“Kheh.”

Gwi Yang let out a low laugh as he straightened his bent body.

Deep cuts were carved across many parts of his body as well.

He had used the members as shields, but those three alone couldn’t have stopped all the incoming Plum Blossom sword qi’s.

Although he had deflected even the sword qi’s that pierced the members’ bodies to some extent, the ones he couldn’t stop left wounds all over him.

But... it wasn’t exactly a bad deal.

For inflicting that level of injury on Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, having the three members’ lives and this degree of injury in return was cheap.

Drip.

From Chung Myung’s long-slit side, blood streamed down without cease.

Chung Myung glanced indifferently at his flank, then pressed the blood channel to staunch the flow.

That motion, so utterly practiced, evoked a strange chill.

Yet even so, Gwi Yang did not miss the faint rise and fall of Chung Myung’s shoulders.

“His breathing is labored.”

Labored breathing means stamina is running low.

Gwi Yang’s mouth, hidden beneath his mask, spread into a wide grin.

“He’s exhausted to the utmost. Isn’t he?”

If Chung Myung were in his right state, no matter how viciously Gwi Yang attacked, he wouldn’t have left a single wound.

This meant that the current Chung Myung was by no means normal.

This being—better called a monster than a man, the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint—had finally been caught in his net.

No matter that he was Mount Hua’s Sword Saint Chung Myung, in that state he could never escape Gwi Yang’s grip.

Snap.

Gwi Yang bit down hard on his lip and drew blood.

As the metallic taste of blood spread in his mouth, his fevered body felt somewhat cooled.

‘No excitement.’

When hunting, one must remain calm until the very end.

Only then can one fully savor the pleasure of sinking teeth into prey’s throat.

Regaining his composure, Gwi Yang nodded towards the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint.

At times like this, a predator always leaves one last strike.

There’s no need to court unnecessary risk.

“Charge.”

No sooner had he spoken than Blood Sword Troops members surged to Gwi Yang’s left and right.

They had clearly seen comrades become Gwi Yang’s meat shields and die, but the dead had already been erased from their minds.

In the Evil Sect, in the Ten Thousand People Clan, this was nothing extraordinary.

“Haaaap!”

The Blood Sword Troops members leaped over the gruesomely mutilated corpses and charged at Chung Myung.

Yu Yiseol rushed forward quickly as if to protect Chung Myung.

“Stand back. I’ll...”

At that moment, Chung Myung grabbed Yu Yiseol’s shoulder as she stepped forward and shoved her violently backward.

Paaaaat!

A flash of bewilderment crossed Yu Yiseol’s face.

But Chung Myung’s already-released sword had ripped through the throats of the charging Blood Sword Troops members in an instant.

“Grrk...”

Even dying, distrust filled their eyes.

‘W-wound...’ Clearly Chung Myung had been injured.

Yet Chung Myung’s sword was no different from before.

Before they could even comprehend the gap between what they expected and reality, their lives were cut short.

Thud. Thud.

Only then did Chung Myung glance briefly at Yu Yiseol.

“This is my place, Sago.”

“Your condition right now...”

Chung Myung’s gaze had already left Yu Yiseol and turned forward.

Yu Yiseol bit her lip hard.

Then Chung Myung spoke in a casual voice.

“I’m exhausted to the utmost.”

“...”

“I’ve been injured; I’m not in normal shape.”

“You...”

“So what of it?”

A twist crossed Chung Myung’s mouth.

Blood-soaked Chung Myung’s lips pulled back, revealing his white teeth in a chilling display.

“That’s war, isn’t it? Isn’t it?”

Gwi Yang’s eyes narrowed slightly at that sight.

Chung Myung looked down at his own flank.

Whether the wound was deeper than it looked, or perhaps due to poisoning, even after cauterizing the blood channel the bleeding would not completely stop.

Chung Myung placed his hand on his side as if to press the wound.

Gwi Yang let out an overt sneer.

“Compared to all the talk, the situation is...”

At that moment, Gwi Yang’s mouth snapped shut.

Sizzle. Chiiiiik!

A acrid smoke seeped from the wound Chung Myung’s hand touched.

Soon the scent of flesh burning brushed past Gwi Yang’s nostrils.

The smile on Gwi Yang’s face beneath the mask vanished as if washed away.

Because the bleeding wouldn’t stop, he was cauterizing the wound by heating it with a hand focusing Yang qi to staunch the flow.

What mad stunt was this?

A chill ran down Gwi Yang’s spine.

“Enough.”

Having cauterized the wound, Chung Myung said with a satisfied smile.

“Let’s continue.”

Watching Chung Myung approach gripping his sword, Gwi Yang’s eyes trembled faintly.

“Wrahchaahhh!”

Jo Gul thrust at the enemies charging him one after another. His momentum hadn’t faltered at all; in fact, it had become sharper than at the start, but his expression was steadily hardening.

“Huh! Huh!”

Almost there.

The damn canyon’s end was finally in sight. Just a little more...

Jo Gul, who had pierced the throat of the attacker, exhaled heavily. His face was already drenched in sweat, and the fingertips gripping his sword began to tremble slightly.

‘Already....’

It felt as if his internal qi had run dry.

It made no sense logically.

Swordsmanship basically emphasizes thrusting. Because of that trait, Jo Gul’s swordsmanship didn’t usually consume much internal qi. In a fight like this, he should be able to repeat it day and night for three days without tiring.

But the impossible had already happened, so he couldn’t deny it.

It wasn’t just the internal qi. Everything that had been filling him felt like it was depleting rapidly. It hadn’t even been a short while since they’d started fighting properly.

‘It’s like this....’

Everything was being consumed at an alarming rate.

Stamina, internal qi, even the mental fortitude he had been confident wouldn’t fall short of anyone.

No sooner had Jo Gul shown signs of fatigue than Yoon Jong charged forward. Yoon Jong’s back was also damp with sweat.

Watching him absentmindedly, Jo Gul lightly tossed his sword into the air and vigorously shook the sweat dampening his hands.

He realized anew how brutal the pressure was to fight at the forefront, to clear the way ahead of everyone else.

“Don’t whine! There are three of us here!”

Yoon Jong and Baek Cheon must be suffering the same pressure. But he couldn’t show such weakness. And if anyone would succumb to the pressure, it would be Baek Cheon rather than them...

Reflexively glancing at Baek Cheon, Jo Gul flinched for a moment. He had expected him to be looking for an opportunity to surge forward, but Baek Cheon was unexpectedly staring somewhere behind.

“Sasuk?”

Jo Gul called out in puzzlement, but Baek Cheon seemed not to hear and just kept staring endlessly to the rear.

“Pht!”

Spitting the blood pooled in his mouth carelessly, Chung Myung glared at Gwi Yang before him.

‘What a mess.’

His body was in such a shambles it was almost out of control.

‘Back then and now, the Tang Family men are the same....’

Those physicians are all the same. They keep saying the same things.

If you’re injured you have to rest. A person’s body doesn’t conveniently heal itself.

‘Who doesn’t know that?’

If he could rest, he would. The problem is he can’t.

Chung Myung had become stronger. In just a few years he had caught up to more than half of his past level.

He had accumulated inner energy bit by bit until he no longer felt it lacking, and his swordsmanship had grown so familiar to this body that it reached a near-perfect state.

But that didn’t mean he’d perfectly recovered his past level.

What he lacked most was internal qi.

In his past life he’d spent nearly seventy years building internal qi. In this life, the time he had to accumulate it wasn’t even ten years.

He had the newly made Heavenly Violet Pill to help, but if a pill could substitute for decades of cultivation, who would bother focusing on internal qi?

This journey breaking through from Southern Island to here at the vanguard was a grueling path even for his past self. He’d endured it with an inexperienced body and inner energy, so it’s no wonder his body was giving out.

He’d focused solely on felling enemies—cut off all concern for everything else for that one purpose—and still ended up like this.

Of course, he could have chosen to fall back to tend wounds and catch his breath. Even choosing that, they would likely have made it this far.

The current Five Swords had grown enough to be proudly presented even against the damned old Mount Hua fellows, and the Heavenly Friends Alliance’s young lord men were doing their parts well enough.

So even if Chung Myung hadn’t overexerted himself, those guys would have led them and they would have reached this place.

Instead, at least thirty more disciples from the Southern Island would have died.

‘Thirty, huh.’

A sudden bitter laugh escaped Chung Myung’s lips.

It wasn’t funny at all.

Who could know better than Chung Myung how trivial human lives are, and how futile it is to scream to save a few lives amid the massive whirlpool of war?

If he were his past self, he would have prioritized preserving his own body rather than overextending himself to save those mere thirty.

If he collapsed, far more than thirty would die. That’s not coldness; it’s a rational judgment.

Yes. That’s certain. But...

‘It would have been better if it weren’t now, Chung Mun sahyung.’

The chuckle kept escaping him, even though he knew it wasn’t the occasion for it.

‘Why should I care about the Southern Island anyway.’

In the end, he had no choice but to admit it.

He had changed Mount Hua. He put the crumbling Mount Hua back on solid rock and instilled ambition in Mount Hua’s disciples who had been soaked in defeatism.

For Chung Myung it was a thorough return to the past.

It was about recreating the Mount Hua he treasured in his memories, the one that still felt within reach.

To be Chung Myung. To be Mount Hua’s Plum Blossom Sword Saint.

But... now he had to admit it. The person who had changed the most in that process wasn’t Mount Hua or those Five Swords guys—it was Chung Myung himself.

Through teaching and scolding those guys, Chung Myung finally came to know.

Human worth isn’t measured by social standing. Even those Chung Myung once sneered at as utterly dull were living their lives desperately—even those used as expendable goods in war and then discarded.

Trivial lives. Lives that end as mere numbers.

Each of those insignificant people had their own life. Even those he dismissed as useless were everything to someone and held immense value.

Chung Myung wanted to protect Chung Mun sahyung.

He wanted to save that damned Tang Bo, and he wanted to see that bastard Chung Jin return to Mount Hua alive and well.

He wanted to somehow send all of Mount Hua’s fellows back to Mount Hua.

If he could have done that, he wouldn’t have hesitated to do anything. Anything.

But now he knew. He’d learned.

Viewed by the vast perspective of the murim world, those utterly insignificant people—the mere numbers—were, to someone, Chung Mun, Tang Bo, and Chung Jin.

Someone would desperately want to protect them like Chung Myung did then. Someone else would be forced to live in agony for the rest of their life because of their deaths.

Thirty. A trivial number, but Chung Myung could no longer regard it as insignificant.

He would have been better off not knowing.

‘Will you sneer?’

Chung Myung suddenly wondered.

If Chung Jin heard this, how would he react? Would he laugh it off as nonsense? Or would he be surprised and laugh that Chung Myung had finally become an enlightened man?

There was no way to know.

Because Chung Jin was already dead.

“Sajil...”

Yu Yiseol’s voice came from behind. Worry subtly seeped through it. No, it was so evident he didn’t even need to strain to hear it.

Worry in Yu Yiseol’s voice.

‘Doesn’t suit him.’

But what suited even less was the man himself who made Yu Yiseol’s voice carry worry.

Chung Myung lightly shifted his grip on the Dark Fragrant Plum Blossom Sword he held.

A sharp sting.

The pain in his leg was terrible. His internal qi had already burned out long ago. Even while running he had tried to muster internal qi, but there was only so much he could do.

The wound he’d painfully cauterized and barely stopped the bleeding from would reopen the moment he moved violently.

The only fortunate thing was that he hadn’t suffered internal injuries, but that wasn’t much consolation.

Nevertheless... Chung Myung raised the corner of his mouth.

“Shall I go ahead?”

The moment Chung Myung leaned slightly forward, Gwi Yang involuntarily took a small step back. His momentum was noticeably reduced compared to before.

Gwi Yang narrowed his eyes suspiciously and checked Chung Myung’s condition several times.

‘What is this?’

The guy was already at his limit. No, he should have been at his limit already. The wounds across his body were such that even a peak master would find movement difficult.

And in reality it would be worse than it looked. The poison smeared on his sword would remain in his body in traces and continue to ravage his insides.

But Chung Myung’s momentum didn’t diminish at all. If anything, it had become more terrifying than at the start. Merely facing him felt like one’s qi being twisted and skin splitting.

‘That’s bluster.’

Gwi Yang bit his lip hard.

Ho Ga-Myung had already said it: when the guy neared his limit he’d put on bluster to hide his condition. So there was no need to be deceived by his words and actions.

Like toying with an injured beast, leisurely...

Fwaaaargh!

But at that moment Chung Myung’s figure suddenly dropped and then popped up like an apparition right before Gwi Yang’s eyes.

Gwi Yang was startled and jerked his sword up.

Kaaang!

The sword he raised reflexively managed to block the incoming Plum Blossom Sword. But the moment steel met steel, Chung Myung’s blade didn’t bounce off—instead it surged toward Gwi Yang’s neck even faster.

Feeling the sword almost touch his throat, Gwi Yang twisted his neck with all his might.

Kwaduk.

He barely avoided having his throat pierced, but couldn’t avoid being cut. The sword sliced about half a finger’s width to the side of the center of his neck, grazing the carotid artery and cutting along the side of his throat.

Gwi Yang staggered back in a daze without having time to feel the pain.

For a moment—if his reaction had been even briefly slower his throat would have been skewered. Realizing he’d just stepped into the gates of hell and escaped, Gwi Yang’s face went pale.

But Gwi Yang had also fought countless battles.

Even so, his eyes tracked Chung Myung precisely. Chung Myung’s leg was slightly bent, and the hand gripping the sword was tensed.

‘He’s coming!’

Gwi Yang snapped awake and straightened his stance.

That beast wouldn’t miss a seized opportunity. Now that Gwi Yang appeared weak, he would surely charge to cut off that lifeline this time.

As Gwi Yang drew up all his internal qi and planted his feet into the ground.

Fwaaaargh!

Chung Myung stomped the ground. Gwi Yang’s hands gripping his sword tightened with all their strength.

‘If I can just block it...’

But soon Gwi Yang’s mind went blank.

Chung Myung, having stomped the ground, moved at high speed.

Not to the front where Gwi Yang stood, but to the rear.

‘What...?’

Leaping backward, Chung Myung let out an outright sneer.

Only then did Gwi Yang realize he’d been thoroughly toyed with, but Chung Myung was already sprinting along the gaping canyon accompanied by Yu Yiseol and Tang Pae.

“You...!”

Crack!

The hand gripping the sword clenched so hard there was a sound like joints dislocating. The sound snapped Gwi Yang back to his senses and he shouted loudly.

“Pursue them! Now!”

“Yes!”

The Blood Sword Troops members, stunned by the unexpected turn and staring blankly at Chung Myung, were startled and gave chase. Catching a glimpse of their retreating backs, Gwi Yang bit his lower lip.

“Goddamn this...”

It had been momentary, but in any case the fact that he’d been thoroughly toyed with and played inside the opponent’s palm shredded Gwi Yang’s pride to pieces.

“Aaaaaargh!”

He screamed like in a fit, then immediately began to give full pursuit to Chung Myung, who was heading toward the canyon’s end.

“Waaaaargh!”

Tang Pae scattered his hidden blades with all his might.

Thrown blades in battle must always be retrieved in mind.

But in Tang Pae’s mind now, there wasn’t even the slightest plan to retrieve those blades.

There was only a single determination: to somehow stop the enemy.

“Take this!”

Puh-eong! Puh-eong! Puh-eong!

And at the same time, black poisonous mist erupted wildly in the gorge where the Blood Sword Troops members were charging.

The Blood Sword Troops members, having warded off the flying blades with full force, gritted their teeth and charged into the poison cloud.

They were resolved to give the enemy no room to retreat, even if they succumbed to the poison.

But waiting for those who broke through the blinding poison was none other than Yu Yiseol’s blade.

Shararak!

Graceful yet utterly decisive, Yu Yiseol’s blade swung in a half-circle toward the charging necks.

Such an elegant strike was hard to be sure anyone could block, even in the ordinary course.

The fate awaiting those who frantically burst out of the poison to meet that blade had already been decided.

Puuuuut!

A fountain of blood burst forth from the cleanly severed neck.

Yu Yiseol’s blade efficiently cut only the enemy’s vital points. Thus those struck by her blade met death without grave wounds.

However, the wound Yu Yiseol’s blade now made was noticeably larger and deeper than the marks her blade usually left.

“Aaaaah!”

Tang Pae surged out beside Yu Yiseol and scattered the concealed weapons in his sleeves as best he could.

Terrible hidden weapons representing the Tang Family—cow-hair needles, the Ghost King Order, and Soul-Severing Sand—mixed and were flung about at will.

But those indiscriminately scattered weapons indeed took effect.

Those who were already hard-pressed to pass through the poison cloud could not possibly block the mad barrage of needles, liquids, and sand.

“Gkwaaaah!”

Needles piercing their bodies, sand and liquids beginning to dissolve skin.

Even the mouths of the Blood Sword Troops members, unparalleled fighters, burst out with screams from the horrific pain those weapons inflicted.

“Let’s go!”

As screams echoed inside the poison cloud, Tang Pae turned and sprinted at full speed without looking back.

“Not yet!”

Just as Chung Myung raised his blade to slaughter those rushing out, something abruptly grabbed Chung Myung’s hand.

Yu Yiseol forcibly seized Chung Myung’s wrist and, before he could react, pulled him along at full force and ran out.

She pulled so hard that pain could be felt in his wrist and shoulder.

“Sago!”

“Follow me!”

Just as Chung Myung was about to speak, a shout burst from Yu Yiseol as if to silence him.

For a moment Chung Myung stood dazed and looked back at Yu Yiseol. He knew he shouldn’t take his eyes off the enemy, but he was that startled.

A shout?

Yu Yiseol?

Chung Myung tried to open his mouth again, but Yu Yiseol was adamant—determined not to let him fight again.

Only then did Chung Myung’s gaze take in the scene ahead in the gorge.

He could see it.

The cursed end of the gorge.

Baek Cheon and Mount Hua’s swordsmen were piercing through that end.

“Waaaargh!”

Kwaaaaang!

A huge white sword-ki detonated. Namgung Do-Wei, having gathered a little strength, was empowering those in front.

The enemies desperately tried to block them, but it was hopelessly uneven.

The gorge was far too narrow and steep to hold back Mount Hua’s thoroughly trained swordsmen.

“Damn! They’re coming again!”

At that moment Tang Pae cried out. Chung Myung reflexively tried to run back, but Yu Yiseol grabbed the collar at the nape of his neck with her sword hand and forcefully pulled him again.

“Run!”

“They’re coming from behind...”

“I know, so run!”

Chung Myung flinched and turned to Yu Yiseol. She glared at him, all wrath revealed.

Chung Myung swore he’d never seen Yu Yiseol make this expression before.

No—he had never even imagined she could wear such an expression. Not once.

“Aaaaah!”

Tang Pae flung hidden weapons in a near-hysteric frenzy.

The hidden weapons stowed in his sleeves were nearly exhausted.

But now was not the time to conserve them. He had to snare their heels even for an instant.

“Tang Family’s young lord!”

“Yes!”

“Grab him and run!”

In that instant Yu Yiseol hurled Chung Myung at Tang Pae and moved toward the rear.

“You damned—!”

The Blood Sword Troops members, smeared with poison and hidden weapons, spurted murderous intent from their eyes and brought their thin swords down on Yu Yiseol.

A thin sword is a weapon whose true power shows when it is thrust rather than slashed.

Even knowing that, their excitement led them to slash for a moment instead of thrusting.

Ka-ga-gang!

Yu Yiseol’s sword, extended at the shortest range, struck aside the incoming Blood Sword Troops member’s blade as if to intercept it.

But the moment blades met and separated, two more blades lunged as if to pierce Yu Yiseol’s entire body.

Under a crushing pressure that felt like her body would be crushed, Yu Yiseol’s sword swung without an inch of hesitation.

Kaaaang!

Sword qi traced a clear circle in the air and struck down the incoming blades at the same time.

At the same time, Yu Yiseol’s eyebrow twitched slightly.

Even though she had parried thin swords far thinner and finer than those used by Mount Hua, a heavy pain settled in her wrist.

Not even pierced nor touched, she felt a stinging like being sliced by a razor on an untouched body.

When life-risking full forces collide.

That battlefield pressure gnawed at Yu Yiseol’s stamina several times faster than usual.

But.

Swaaeaaak!

Still, Yu Yiseol’s blade swung unwaveringly, pushed aside enemy blades, and shot toward their necks with unrivaled speed.

Cheng! Cheng! Kwa-deuk!

Two were blocked, but one was not.

The sensation of a blade cutting through flesh and lodging into bone—before she could even fully feel that horrifying sense, Yu Yiseol pulled out the blade, kicked off the ground, and instantly retreated.

“Ugh!”

“You bitch!”

Sparks lit in the eyes of the Blood Sword Troops members.

Just as they were about to charge again, fine sand like dust was scattered above their heads.

The Blood Sword Troops members flinched and swung their blades to fend off the falling sand above their heads.

But no matter how much they swung their blades, they couldn’t stop all of the falling sand.

The sand that evaded the scattered blades bit into their skin.

“Grrk...”

A Blood Sword Troops member overcome by the surging poison coughed up dark, frothy blood.

One who followed shoved the faltering squad member off a cliff and, emitting a beastly howl, charged at Yu Yiseol.

“Dieeeee!”

A blade fell fiercely from above. One could tell without experiencing it how potent the energy it carried was.

A strike that seemed to put everything on a single blow. As Yu Yiseol bit her lip, ready to meet that strike.

Paaaaaat!

A sword qi sprayed past right beside Yu Yiseol’s neck and instantly pierced the neck of the Blood Sword Troops member who was charging.

The attacker was flung backwards as if hit by a shell from that residual force.

Blood gushed like a fountain from his gaping neck, flooding the gorge with crimson.

Paaaat! Paaaapat!

Blades flew in succession and pierced the necks of the Blood Sword Troops members who charged on without losing momentum despite their comrade’s death.

Seeing that, Yu Yiseol bit her lip and swung her blade again.

‘That’s my share!’

Her blade sliced the air like a ray and buried itself in the neck of a charging Blood Sword Troops member.

Puuuuk!

The sound of the blade tearing flesh. As she sensed that sound overlapping with something else, her gaze instinctively dropped downward.

The blade, stained with blood after penetrating the chest of the Blood Sword Troops member she had cut, suddenly popped out and flew toward Yu Yiseol’s solar plexus.

Yu Yiseol’s eyes widened in an instant.

From behind, it would have been blatantly obvious.

But for someone pouring all their power into striking the enemy, there is no more vicious trick than this.

Sensing it was too late to react, Yu Yiseol could only watch the blade aiming to pierce her belly—when another blade-ki flew into her sight.

Kaaaang!

A red sword qi, flying like lightning, knocked away the blade that had been attacking Yu Yiseol in an instant.

The deflected blade cleaved the Blood Sword Troops member’s body in two with one stroke, and through the spurting blood, Gwi Yang’s face twisted like a fiend as he surged forward in a frenzy.

“You damned bastard!”

It’s sickening.

How else could one describe Gwi Yang now?

The Captains of the Ten Thousand People Clan—one would not dare take them as subordinates unless one were Jang Ilso.

Gwi Yang, one of those captains, doggedly pursued as if to prove that public appraisal of them wasn’t exaggerated but even understated.

“Get out of the way!”

But to Gwi Yang, someone like Yu Yiseol blocking his path was of no concern.

His gaze was fixed on Chung Myung behind Yu Yiseol—the wounded, panting predator.

Everything has its time.

This moment was the most decisive. If he let that one slip now, he’d have to go through a grueling process again.

Moreover, his certainty that he could definitely capture the man at the end of that process was crumbling now.

So now, now he must finish it.

But Yu Yiseol had no intention of letting Gwi Yang go easily.

Swaaeaaak!

A paradox: swift and sharp, yet supremely smooth.

Yu Yiseol’s blade, embodying that paradox in reality, swung smoothly and sliced Gwi Yang’s body horizontally.

Ka-gang!

Gwi Yang flicked aside the flying blade in an instant without even seeming to breathe.

Then, as if brushing off an annoyance, he aimed to slash Yu Yiseol’s torso, but the rebounded blade, as if expected, spun a circle in the air and came again to cut Gwi Yang’s neck.

“Ugh!”

A short groan burst from Gwi Yang’s mouth.

Kang! Kaang! Kang!

Gwi Yang’s and Yu Yiseol’s blades collided in the air several times.

“You...!”

A murderous rage boiled in Gwi Yang’s eyes.

This blade clung to him tenaciously. No matter how many times he struck it away, it flew back again as if it had anticipated it.

A blade resembling Chung Myung’s, yet clearly of a different grain—that blade was scratching Gwi Yang’s nerves raw.

“You damned—!”

Paaaaaah!

Just as Gwi Yang was about to unleash his rage, a lightning-like sword qi slammed down explosively from behind Yu Yiseol’s shoulder.

Gwi Yang twisted his body violently to the side, but the flying sword qi still pierced his flank without fail.

Blood gurgled up into Gwi Yang’s mouth.

Though his organs weren’t ruptured, being pierced and the force driving in shook his insides thoroughly.

Gwi Yang glared bloodshot as he looked behind Yu Yiseol.

Chung Myung, standing just behind Yu Yiseol, stared at him with the eyes of a predator eyeing its prey.

‘Hunt?’

To be hunted? He?

“You...”

As Gwi Yang could no longer contain his boiling fury and was about to howl.

Hwaaaaaak!

A vast river of Plum Blossom petals flooded his sight.

In front of him?

No, far away!

Plum Blossom petals, rising like rapids from the gorge’s end, swept away those who resisted in an instant.

And the flowing sword qi, as if a river pouring into the sea, passed over the narrow valley and spread out across the vast land.

Which meant...

“It’s open!”

Baek Cheon.

In his eyes, having broken the last resistance, the vast expanse of land appeared. The long, long gorge had finally revealed its end.

Seeing that, Baek Cheon clenched his fist and shouted at the top of his lungs.

“Everyone run! Get out of the gorge!”

“Let’s gooooo!”

“Waaaaah!”

At Jo Gul’s shout, even Yoon Jong responded with a roar.

In an instant, the morale of Southern Island’s disciples surged a hundredfold as they all shouted till their throats hurt and sprinted full speed toward the opened path.

Those who had been camped outside the gorge eyed the scene ahead with startled expressions.

The enemies who had walked into the gorge of their own accord and leapt into the poison like rats were now clawing their way out of that slick trap.

How many had been stationed inside the gorge to stop them? Yet those bastards turned them all into corpses and finally reached this place.

“Sto-stop them!”

Someone cried out in urgent panic.

But even the one shouting knew it was meaningless. How could they possibly stop those who had not been stopped before reaching here?

At that moment, the one who surged forward first drove home that thought.

“Hyaaahhh!”

Jo Gul, leaping like a maddened stoat, formed dozens of sword shadows and pierced the rushing attackers in an instant.

Thud!

A vast world poured into Jo Gul’s eyes as he landed.

“We’re out! Damn it! We broke through!”

Joy surged through his whole body. Those who followed him likewise rushed out of the gorge one after another.

“Waaaaaaah!”

“Damn it!”

“We’re alive! We’re alive!”

Those especially excited by the situation were the Southern Island disciples.

A narrow gorge, enemies still blocking the way no matter how they broke through, and even the Ten Thousand People Clan trailing behind.

It seemed utterly hopeless. More than half of them thought this gorge would be their grave. Yet to be alive and set foot on wide open ground again filled them with indescribable emotion.

Outward, further outward.

Those who had regained the light ran, drunk on joy.

“Commander.”

The aides looked at Ho Ga-Myung standing beside them with eyes full of various meanings.

“Hmm.”

A low, composed murmur escaped Ho Ga-Myung’s mouth as he had entered the gorge and watched the situation ahead.

“They got through.”

There was no other way to think about it. His plan to seal the gorge and annihilate the enemy had utterly collapsed.

He didn’t know whether to praise the enemy or blame his own shortcomings.

And Ho Ga-Myung’s choice was neither.

“Don’t stop, keep moving! The enemy is still behind us!”

“Yes, sasuk!”

At Baek Cheon’s shout from behind, Jo Gul answered at the top of his lungs and dashed forward.

‘End of the day, he’s incredible!’

He was briefly carried away and forgot the situation, but Baek Cheon remained cool-headed. With someone like him commanding, even the Four Sects Alliance couldn’t stop the Mount Hua party.

Perhaps even escaping this Southern Lands region might not be as difficult as they feared.

“Where! Where are we going, sahyung!”

“Are you asking me? Run north!”

“Where’s north?”

“Damn it! Follow the way I’m going!”

“Yes!”

Jo Gul quickly fell in behind Yoon Jong, who was running ahead, and looked back to signal the Southern Island disciples the direction.

“Everyone, this way...”

But then.

A strange sight unfolded before their eyes.

Contrasting with the hellish gorge, things began to stir and reveal themselves all across the vast forest ahead.

‘What...’

Could it be an ambush?

Even amid bewilderment, they quickly turned their heads and analyzed.

Isn’t it an ambush that strikes when people are most relaxed? It’s a sweat-inducing move, but calmly considered, they could handle this.

Besides, those bastards revealed themselves too early and their positions were already exposed, so they could prepare.

Jo Gul was about to warn them to beware of the ambush ahead when something caught his eye.

In hands revealed across the forest were horn bows.

In an instant, a scream like thunder erupted from Jo Gul’s mouth.

“Get downwwww!”

But qi-infused arrows flew indiscriminately even faster than that shout.

Thud-thud-thud!

The powerful arrows, launched at tremendous speed, mercilessly pierced the Southern Island disciples who had been shouting in joy.

“Ugh...”

“Aaaaaaaah!”

One whose face was pierced fell with a stifled moan, and another, impaled by an arrow, screamed in agony.

“Damn it!”

Jo Gul swore, unable to contain the rising rage.

Those who had broken through that hellish gorge reduced to this so futilely!

“Sahyung!”

“It is still okay! It’s only a few!”

The bow is a weapon difficult for most martial folk to use.

Even in the Four Sects Alliance, overflowing with numbers, there couldn’t be many who could shoot force-infused arrows at speeds that skilled fighters couldn’t even react to.

Especially considering they were mobilized suddenly. At most maybe ten. If they stayed calm and responded...

Then Yoon Jong widened his eyes. His gaze was directed at the sky.

The vast sky they finally faced after breaking through the gorge was... turning black.

It wasn’t storm clouds. It was hundreds, thousands of arrows flying out of the forest.

A cry of despair burst from Yoon Jong’s mouth.

“D-dodge! Right now!”

But they couldn’t.

The Southern Island disciples were half-stuck at the gorge’s exit, unable to fully get out. Those still trapped in the narrow gorge couldn’t free their bodies. And that rain of arrows was targeting them mercilessly.

Baek Cheon and Namgung Do-Wei reflexively leapt up and swung their swords frantically to deflect the arrows.

They weren’t the same powerful arrows as before, but there was no way just the two of them could fend off the arrows pouring down like rain.

Nearly half of the incoming arrows maintained their speed and rained down over the Southern Island disciples’ heads.

“Ugh!”

“Aaaaah!”

The poison-tipped arrows pierced their bodies without mercy.

Wretched screams burst out all around. Because the attack came suddenly when they had relaxed, the damage was severe.

Seeing comrades fall impaled by arrows, fear spread quickly in the eyes of the other Southern Island disciples.

“S-sahyung! Are you okay...”

Thud-thud.

“...Ugh.”

An arrow flying horizontally lodged into the temple of one who was frantically looking around. It was a powerful arrow of a different class than the regular ones from above. The Southern Island disciples could only be helpless.

In an instant more than ten people collapsed.

Those who had withstood all manner of enemy attacks without major losses suddenly suffered more than a dozen casualties from a single strike.

They had barely broken through the hellish gorge!

An unparalleled despair blossomed in everyone’s chest.

“We have to, h-hurry out!”

“What are you doing up front? Keep running!”

“Don’t stop! The Ten Thousand People Clan is coming from the rear! Move fast!”

At the gorge’s exit, the Southern Island disciples began to entangle and trample each other.

Some couldn’t run because the way ahead was blocked or they didn’t know when a powerful arrow might strike; others had to get out of the gorge by any means. None would yield.

In the end chaos erupted as the Southern Island’s disciples shoved and pulled at each other.

“What are you doing, you bastards!”

Southern Island Sect’s senior disciple Gwak Hwan-So shouted.

“Don’t panic! It’s just arrows! If you calmly deflect them, it’s not impossible to stop them! You are the Southern Island disciples!”

Another rain of arrows rose into the sky.

Gwak Hwan-So gritted his teeth and leapt up. Not comparable to Baek Cheon or Namgung Do-Wei, but he thought he had to step up and try to handle the situation.

As Baek Cheon and Namgung Do-Wei followed him, ready to lift themselves again, someone suddenly sprang over his head.

“Sect leader!”

Geum Yang-Baek and the elders had already rushed forward, despite their frail bodies, to shield the disciples from the arrow rain.

Rat-tat-tat! Ratatatatat!

The clamor of arrows and blades colliding rang out loudly. Their weak bodies had arrows embedded here and there from momentary misses, but the Southern Island elders didn’t utter a groan.

This time the Southern Island Sect’s disciples gritted their teeth and deflected the falling arrows. Not a complete defense, but the casualties were clearly reduced compared to before.

Sssing!

Geum Yang-Baek violently swung his sword and knocked away a powerful arrow. The shock stung his wrist. This arrow was certainly of a different caliber than the previous ones.

“Sect leader!”

“Acting Sect Leader! I’ll take this spot!”

“Yes!”

Baek Cheon, without even nodding, charged forward.

To resolve this, they had to quickly deal with those firing the powerful arrows. If they couldn’t kill them, they couldn’t advance from here.

“Yoon Jong! Jo Gul! Spread out and take the ones shooting arrows!”

“Yes, sasuk!”

Jo Gul and Yoon Jong replied at once as if they had expected it. The two rushed toward where the powerful arrows had been shot. Or tried to.

Kraaaaang!

A booming blast like thunder erupted. At the same time, something dark flew toward the Southern Island Sect’s disciples.

‘Cannons?’

An elder reflexively swatted a flying shell with his sword. But if a sword could stop a shell, what reason would cannonry have to exist?

Boom!

The sword split in two as the dark shell slammed into the elder’s body. There was no time to scream. The dying elder was flung back, spewing blood from seven orifices.

Kraaaang! Kraaaang!

The blasts continued in succession.

Of course, there weren’t many. No matter the Four Sects Alliance or that this was Guangdong territory, they couldn’t have mobilized dozens of banned cannons. So there were only a few cannon pieces spewing fire.

But the combination of those few cannon pieces, the simultaneous rain of arrows, and powerful arrows that seemed aimed to snipe key commanders made it little different from hell.

Arrows flew over Jo Gul’s head and continuously struck the Southern Island disciples.

It’s not hard for him to run forward and fell enemies, but in doing so those behind him could suffer near-total destruction.

To win they must attack. But to attack they’d have to rip their own flesh off first. What else could they do in a situation like this?

“Jo Gul!”

Baek Cheon’s roar cut into Jo Gul’s ears amidst the chaos.

“Don’t hesitate!”

“B-but...!”

“I’m right behind you!”

No more words were needed.

Baek Cheon’s eyes were full of stubborn certainty. The moment Jo Gul saw it, his feet had already driven into the ground.

Certainty began to fill Jo Gul’s eyes as well.

He was Mount Hua’s assault captain. Holding back did not suit him. The way he could help them was to fell the attackers as quickly as possible.

“You bastards!”

Jo Gul shouted fiercely and shot forward like a beam of light.

Kwaaaaang!

Kwaaaaaaaang!

The roar of shells being fired echoed thunderously into the gorge. Even with the very limited visibility, it was not hard to make out what was happening outside.

One of Southern Island’s elders, struggling to block incoming shells despite being poisoned and unable to properly use his internal energy, took a direct hit in midair and was flung into the gorge.

Seeing that gruesome sight, one of Ho Ga-Myung’s aides unconsciously clenched his fist tightly.

‘As expected.’

His gaze turned to Ho Ga-Myung beside him. Any strategist would be chilled by this situation.

But Ho Ga-Myung showed no trace of excitement even now. He simply focused, as if refusing to miss a single detail of what unfolded before him.

Each time like this, he found himself marveling at Ho Ga-Myung again and again.

Because he had unveiled some great stratagem?

No, that wasn’t the reason.

In truth, the strategy itself wasn’t particularly remarkable. Projecting fire onto those exiting a narrow place is basic, basic stuff. Hardly something to call military art.

What was truly impressive was his meticulousness in preparing for what lay behind the enemy, even though they had walked into the poisonous snare of the gorge of their own accord.

If it had been someone other than Ho Ga-Myung, they would have sent those cannons and archers... no, perhaps every available unit straight into the gorge.

To turn as many inside that narrow gorge into lost souls as possible.

But looking back now, in a narrow, winding gorge, bows couldn’t demonstrate their true power, and cannons couldn’t be fired effectively. They would instead have been hampered by the cramped terrain filled with their own men.

Even if they positioned forces above the gorge, firing cannons down from that height was impossible. Arrows shot from above would lose their power by the time they hit the ground, becoming nothing but waste.

If those passing through the gorge were ordinary soldiers, unimbued arrows might suffice, but against trained murim warriors, arrows without internal qi would be useless.

Thanks to those useless forces encamped outside the gorge, they were now inflicting devastating damage on the enemies.

It may not be entirely so, but at a glance the damage inflicted by a single volley of arrows and bombardment seemed greater than what they had suffered inside the gorge.

“C-commander.”

At that moment another aide stammered out.

“D-did you foresee all of this and position them accordingly?”

Ho Ga-Myung frowned slightly. The aide who had asked the question went pale and quickly bowed his head.

“S-sorry.”

This was hardly the time for idle Q&A. Knowing that and yet failing to suppress his curiosity was a clear mistake.

But then Ho Ga-Myung spoke.

“Hit the enemy’s weak spot.”

“...Yes?”

“That’s the basics.”

Ho Ga-Myung’s eyes remained fixed on the enemies.

Attacking from advantageous terrain is a basic tenet of military strategy, and targeting the enemy’s weak points is another basic principle.

Others were focused only on what was before them; he was focused solely on what lay behind.

“When you say weak spot...?”

But the aides did not fully understand his words. A weak point—what did that have to do with this situation?

The enemy’s weaknesses were their lack of combat experience and their small numbers.

“What problem arises when they enter the gorge?”

“They haven’t experienced that...”

“No.”

Ho Ga-Myung quickly continued.

“Their formation gets stretched out.”

“Th-that means the number that can attack at once decreases, making it difficult to fully utilize their strength.”

Ho Ga-Myung’s eye twitched again.

The one who had answered thoughtlessly quickly bowed his head deeply.

“A head is meant for thinking. Who benefits when few number face few number has already been proven inside the gorge? This gorge is not advantageous to us.”

“...I was foolish.”

“There is only one advantage we gain from their stretched formation.”

Ho Ga-Myung’s gaze fixed on one place: Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, who was holding back Gwi Yang’s pursuit alongside Yu Yiseol.

“They cannot exist in two places at once.”

“Ah...”

“A strength is the absence of a weakness. Conversely, a weakness is the absence of a strength. The moment the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint is not present, their weakness is exposed.”

“...”

“Thoroughly targeting the enemy’s weakness is the basics of strategy.”

“So you knew that fellow would be in the rear in advance...?”

“No.”

“Yes?”

“It doesn’t matter which. If he had been in front, it would have been impossible to damage him with such arrows and shells, but the rear would have been thoroughly toyed with by Gwi Yang.”

“Ah...”

“Now it’s simply reversed. Since he’s at the back, even the Blood Sword Troops can’t exert themselves, but that allows us to break the front.”

Only then did those who understood Ho Ga-Myung’s idea nod unconsciously. It sounded obvious once spoken, yet no one here had thought of it—except Ho Ga-Myung.

Ho Ga-Myung closed his eyes briefly.

He hadn’t bothered to say this to teach them. He spoke it aloud to himself to reaffirm his own resolve.

‘No need for clever stratagems.’

No, to be honest, he didn’t know how to employ flashy schemes.

If Jang Ilso had been here, he would have used completely different moves: collapsing the cliff in unimaginable ways to bury them at once, or turning the earth over to crush them.

But that is the sort of thing Jang Ilso could do. Ho Ga-Myung could not conceive such methods. So he would delve in, more stubbornly adhering to the basics.

‘This war is one of attrition.’

He had no way of knowing how the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint interpreted this war, but in Ho Ga-Myung’s judgment it was a thorough war of attrition.

So rather than taking reckless risks, he would nibble them down. By steadily reducing them person by person, even the big fish that was the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint would eventually be caught in the net he set.

Ho Ga-Myung’s gaze turned to the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint. Fatigue was evident on his face.

‘He must know, of course.’

Ho Ga-Myung did not overestimate himself. He did not think Chung Myung would be unaware of what he sought.

Yet the reason Ho Ga-Myung did not hesitate was because the world contains the saying ‘Small number of people cannot deal with large number’—the many cannot be defeated by the few.

There are things that even knowledge cannot stop. There are cases where awareness yields no remedy. That’s why people say the outcome of a war is decided before it even begins.

A war that cannot be won.

That the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint himself had leapt into such a situation was his limitation, and a weakness those who called themselves the righteous had no choice but to bear.

Ho Ga-Myung cast a quick glance at Chung Myung. The pair of dark flickering eyes suddenly cooled.

This was no time to be intoxicated with victory. He had to make the next move before that fellow stirred.

“Nothing has changed.”

Everything still existed smoothly within his awareness.

Chung Myung bit his lip, unable to hold back his anger.

‘Ho Ga-Myung.’

He glared fiercely at the distant Ho Ga-Myung. He wanted to rush over and cut that man’s throat, but the wily fox would never allow him such proximity.

The unease Chung Myung had felt toward Ho Ga-Myung began to take shape in this moment.

Chung Myung’s explosive power and unexpected ingenuity were disastrously mismatched with those who, unperturbed by circumstances, did their duty with cold thoroughness.

‘Damn!’

Chung Myung glanced slightly behind him.

Southern Island Sect was resisting desperately, but it was mere endurance.

But withdrawing was difficult. The moment they charged out chasing the enemy, those who hadn’t escaped the gorge would be exposed helplessly to the rain of attacks.

‘There aren’t enough blades!’

Not just holding the line, but blades that could move alone and cleave the enemy...!

Chung Myung grabbed Yu Yiseol by the shoulder and pulled her abruptly.

When Yu Yiseol looked at him with startled eyes, Chung Myung charged back toward the rear and shouted.

“Go quickly and help that side, sago!”

“Sajil!”

“Go!”

Only then did Yu Yiseol fully see the situation. She understood why Chung Myung had shoved her. The side that needed help now was there—more than Chung Myung himself.

But...

She saw Chung Myung’s leg flinch for an instant as he pushed off the ground. It could no longer be fully hidden.

Both were in danger. Neither could be abandoned.

Then what on earth...

“I told you to go!”

Chung Myung barked loudly at the confused Yu Yiseol, even though Gwi Yang might charge again at any moment.

Seeing her sajil’s bloodshot eyes, she clenched her fists until her knuckles bled.

She knew.

She had learned. She had been taught. She had rehearsed it.

She knew, to the point of hating it, exactly where a swordsman must run when faced with a choice where neither side could be taken.

The teachings her sect had given her now felt like a sharp dagger. It pained her that she could never turn away from that learning.

Gritting her teeth, Yu Yiseol turned and, as if shaking off lingering regrets, shot off like a beam of light.

Seeing that, Chung Myung gave the same order to Tang Pae.

“Go there together and help!”

“Yes!”

Tang Pae also ran back without complaint.

After sending the two off, Chung Myung stared at Gwi Yang. Gwi Yang flicked the sword in his hand and advanced slowly.

“I thought you’d at least say thank you?”

“That would be for me to hear, rather.”

“Heh heh heh heh.”

A gleam of smug satisfaction flashed in Gwi Yang’s eyes.

He wasn’t wrong. It was true they had given time to tidy the situation, but that was not necessarily disadvantageous to Gwi Yang.

Why stop a wounded prey that had fallen from the pack and remained alone?

He didn’t care about the others’ situations. The only thing that held his interest was Chung Myung before him.

He could see it: the convulsing wounded leg, the side where hastily applied pressure had split open and bled again.

Not only that. His whole body was riddled with countless small wounds, and his face was sallow as if from internal injury.

He had surely already lost a considerable amount of blood.

In such a state, even the greatest warrior in the land would be unable to display even a fraction of his true power.

A short breath escaped Chung Myung’s lips.

Honestly, this was getting a bit too much.

Perhaps from blood loss, his vision blurred. The hand gripping his sword had lost strength. He was used to pain, but the helplessness that comes after being utterly exhausted never grew familiar.

But he still had to fight.

“A weary business, isn’t it.”

Gwi Yang sneered as he closed the distance.

“To know you must retreat and yet be unable to retreat—”

“...”

“—even though you plainly know it’s the road to death.”

Despite the blatant mockery, Chung Myung instead gave a faint laugh.

“Do you know why you’re weak?”

“What?”

Gwi Yang narrowed his eyes at the unexpected question. Chung Myung mocked him and said:

“Because you ran when you wanted to run.”

“...”

“The reason I’m strong is simple too. When I wanted to run, I couldn’t.”

Be it at any time.

They didn’t understand what it meant to be strong. They didn’t know what he had truly wanted to become.

Chung Myung was still being pursued.

One who had never once retreated. One who had been stronger than anyone in the world.

“Talk your nonsense in hell.”

Gwi Yang, flanked by the Blood Sword Troops members, charged in at once.

In front of Chung Myung, who met the charge without backing down, the back of the one he had always watched flickered dimly.

Puuuk!

“Aaaaaah!”

An arrow sank into his chest. A Southern Island disciple screamed, unable to bear the pain.

They could, of course, bat away ordinary falling arrows. They hadn’t trained carelessly until now.

But when arrowheads struck the sword barriers the elders had formed, shattered, and sprayed outwards, there was nothing they could do to stop them embedding everywhere.

“Kuh, kuuh...”

A burning pain in his chest drained strength from his entire body. He sank down clutching his sternum.

“Let go!”

At that moment, as if sensing it, Tang Soso dashed over, ripped his hand away, and checked the wound.

“Miss... back...”

Another shard of a broken arrow flew and lodged in Tang Soso’s back as she sat before the fallen man. She didn’t flinch and began to probe his wound.

“Hold on!”

Clenching her teeth, Tang Soso pressed a suture needle to the injured man’s wound and plunged it in without hesitation.

Crunch!

At the same time, the lodged arrowhead burst through his back.

“Waaaah!”

A desperate scream tore out of him, but Tang Soso held him down and stuffed antidote and a styptic into the gaping wound. Her hands were soon stained dark with blood.

To the eye, it was an exceedingly brutal method. But there was no other choice now. Left as is, the arrowheads would sink further, twist through the body, and might reach the heart.

If there wasn’t time to carefully split the wound and remove the arrowhead, this way was actually safer.

Having quickly bound the wound, Tang Soso left the semi-unconscious man and lunged to the side to treat another who groaned with an arrow lodged in his throat.

But before she could reach him, he pulled the arrow from his own throat. Blood gushed out in choked waves.

“Ah, no!”

Tang Soso screamed and desperately clamped the wound with both hands.

“Kk, kkrrk...”

Foamy blood bubbled up in his mouth and the color drained from the man’s face.

“Hold on! Just hold on a bit longer! I’ll save you, so hang on!”

But despite her cry, life drained quickly from his eyes. The light of living faded.

Tang Soso’s fingertips trembled faintly. The blood she felt was still so hot, yet he was no longer among the living.

She squeezed her eyes shut and leapt up. She ran toward another falling figure. She didn’t want to cry, but tears kept coming at every chance. She could barely see ahead.

But there was no time to wipe them or to grieve.

Powerless. Miserably so.

No matter what she did, she couldn’t save the dying. There was no way to stop the light going out of the eyes of those who looked at her, pleading to be saved.

Had she known that being a physician was this torturous, she would never have taken up medicine.

Yet she ignored despair and kept moving. Among all those here, she was the only physician.

Paaahng!

At that moment, a massive iron bolt, loaded with enormous internal qi, hurtled toward her at tremendous speed. Tang Soso’s eyes widened as she sensed it. Before she could react, someone streaked in like lightning and smashed the bolt aside.

“Sasuk!”

It was Baek Cheon. He gritted his teeth and stared toward where the bolt had come from. He wanted to dash over and tear those damned bastards limb from limb, but there was no way he could leave here now.

‘Yoon Jong, Jo Gul! Hurry...!’

He had no choice but to trust those who had gone and hold the line.

“They’re coming again!”

Baek Cheon’s gaze snapped up to the sky. The Southern Island elders who had just held off the bombardment hadn’t yet regained their footing, and more arrows were already pouring down.

‘No!’

Baek Cheon leapt into the air. His eyes were bloodshot. Even without help he had to do something; otherwise, he’d be forced to watch people die with their eyes open again.

He squeezed out the last of his internal qi from his dantian. But before he could push it into his sword, someone beside him soared even higher.

“Samae!”

At that familiar silhouette Baek Cheon couldn’t help but shout. Yu Yiseol, leaping up like a phoenix, swung her sword at the raining arrows.

Sshhhh!

With a sound like steel scraping silk, Yu Yiseol’s sword drew a great circle in the air, like a full moon.

The Moon Maiden Sword at its peak.

The sword qi formed by that arc acted like a huge umbrella in a storm, stopping the deluge of arrows.

“Samae, be careful! There’s bombardment incoming!”

Baek Cheon shouted urgently. At that moment something streaked past him at incredible speed.

‘Dagger?’

A dagger flew like a vortex cutting through the air. Whirling fiercely, it shot straight into the forest ahead.

“Aaaaaah!”

It was distant and faint, but a scream was definitely heard.

That dagger must have achieved something. Tang Pae, who had arrived a bit later than the dagger I fired, rolled to avoid the rain of arrows and shouted.

“I can keep the cannon fire in check! Just stop the arrows, Acting Sect Leader!”

“Yes!”

Baek Cheon landed lightly and felt strength returning to his body.

Only two people had joined, yet the air seemed to clear in an instant. It was a moment that proved how much power a single master could bring on the martial world.

The others weren’t idle either.

“Damn it! Stop pushing forward and stick to the cliff face!”

Im So-Byeong shouted the command.

“Mount Hua people and young lords, don’t waste strength on arrows—deal only with the cannonballs! Elders, while stopping arrows, cling so the person behind has their chest against your back! Then you only need to defend above—idiots!”

A command given in the chaos becomes a landmark by its mere presence. It didn’t matter whether the one issuing it was the leader of the Evil Sect or not. To save themselves, everyone instinctively followed the order.

Paaaah!

Yu Yiseol touched down and charged forward at tremendous speed.

“Samae!”

Baek Cheon called urgently, but Yu Yiseol didn’t even turn her head.

Paaaaaang!

Perhaps perceiving Yu Yiseol’s movement as a threat, a bolt at terrifying speed flew at her. But she, still running, sliced her sword and struck the incoming bolt.

Clang!

Metal clashed with metal in a sharp ringing. The deflected bolt sliced along Yu Yiseol’s shoulder as it passed.

But Yu Yiseol seemed not to care about the wound... rather, she ran as if unaware she’d been cut. She looked unbearably urgent.

Sensing something wrong, Baek Cheon took a step instinctively and then stopped. He bit the inside of his cheek.

Bodies were piling on the ground. The Southern Island disciples’ and elders’ corpses were studded with arrows like hedgehogs.

What he had to do was protect everyone here—reduce the number of people ending up like that by even one.

His hand holding the sword trembled as his internal qi waned, but there was no room for weak words.

Kwaaaang! Kwaaaaang!

At the bombardment roaring at their ears again, Baek Cheon lashed out at the incoming shells as if in a frenzy.

Blood seeped from his bitten lip. But Jo Gul felt no pain.

There were archers ahead firing arrows like rain. Charging there and cutting them down wasn’t that difficult.

But Jo Gul suppressed that urge as if driving a blade into his thigh.

‘Where are you!’

He was definitely nearby. One of the archers firing those brutal bolts! If they didn’t deal with him, the main force couldn’t move.

‘Where are you!’

Jo Gul swiftly scanned the surroundings. The razor-edge sense of Tanger spread rapidly in all directions.

A moment later.

“Found you!”

Jo Gul bent his waist and surged forward like an arrow.

The archer, realizing his position had been exposed, fired arrow after arrow at Jo Gul—a terrifying rapid shot. Dozens of bolts rained down on him at once.

But Jo Gul’s speed was unmatched. His sword moved as if splitting light, knocking every flying arrow aside.

“You bastard!”

Even as the attacks poured in, Jo Gul’s speed didn’t falter, and bewilderment crept into the masked archer’s eyes.

But only for a moment—realizing rapid fire alone wouldn’t be enough, the archer poured his mood and inner energy into his arrows.

Wooooooong!

When internal qi is put into a blade it resonates. The arrows in his hand began to writhe like living carp. Nocking one, he pulled the steel-twisted bowstring to the breaking point.

Kkrrrk!

With the bow drawn to its limit he took aim at Jo Gul.

But Jo Gul, knowing a bolt laden with tremendous internal qi was aimed at him, didn’t dodge; he ran straight toward it.

As the distance closed so their eyes met.

Paang!

The archer released the string. The arrow spun fiercely and flew straight for Jo Gul.

Kwaaaaah!

Air coiled toward the arrowhead, creating a vortex in the air that resembled an enraged dragon.

Jo Gul, resisting it, drew his sword hand back over his left shoulder.

“Haap!”

With a shout, the edge of his Plum Blossom Sword struck the incoming arrow with all his might.

Kaaaaaang!

A metallic sound, almost like an explosion, rang sharply.

Even with all his force in the blade, the internal qi-filled arrow did not bounce off; it advanced as if to bite into Jo Gul’s sword. The arrowhead’s spinning against the blade made a chilling grinding sound.

A normal iron sword would have shattered instantly under that impact. But the Plum Blossom Sword, infused with the Tang Family’s essence, stubbornly withstood the spinning, burrowing arrow.

And at that moment.

“Taaap!”

With a fierce shout, Jo Gul thrust his left hand toward the sword. His hand, stained green with Mount Hua’s Bamboo Leaf Palm technique, slammed hard against the opposite side of the blade where the arrow had struck.

Kwaaaang!

The added shock to the sword twisted the arrow’s trajectory. Still, its remaining force grazed Jo Gul’s side.

Jo Gul’s flank bore a hollowed, semicircular wound, and the left hand that struck the blade was cut so deeply bone showed.

The price bought him only the distance of about three paces to the enemy.

“Ugh!”

The archer quickly leapt back, drew another arrow from his quiver, and nocked it.

Just as he raised the bow to aim at his opponent, a streak of light flew into his sight.

Paaaah!

A swift sword pierced the archer’s throat in an instant.

“Gak...”

The archer gave a strangled sound and crumpled with a look of disbelief.

‘Couldn’t even see it...’

Thud.

Jo Gul, having pulled the sword from the archer’s neck, clenched his blood-soaked left hand and drove his foot into the ground.

‘Where next!’

The mischievous, lively expression that was always on his face had been replaced by a cold, blade-like countenance.
